**Referenced globals:** DAT_002074a8 (svc-A semaphore), DAT_0020749c (cmd word), DAT_002074c4 (svc 0x597 bound-flag), client node 0x25bc00, req buffer 0x25ba80/0x25baa4, status word 0x25bbc0
**Referenced strings:** trace fmts 0x24e928/0x24e948/0x24e960/0x24e970/0x24e980
**Referenced opcodes:** — (SIF-RPC layer, below SN@P)
**State vars:** ram0x00207490 (debug verbosity gate)

## Behavioral explanation
EE→IOP SIF-RPC client wrapper. One of a family (0x00100838/b70/e88/1010a8/101388/101580) that binds distinct RPC service IDs into per-service client nodes and issues synchronous commands, each guarded by a shared net-RPC mutex. This member binds **service 0x80000597** (client node `0x25bc00`) and sends a **variable-length string request** (`param_2`, copied up to 0x100 bytes into the shared request buffer at `0x25baa4`, plus scalar `param_3`) in a 300-byte send buffer, then copies a fixed **0x24-byte result structure** back into the caller's `param_1` and returns a 4-byte status word. Shape (string in → address/result-struct out, 300-byte fixed request) is consistent with a **name/URL resolve or DNAS lookup request**; the exact service semantics are a Hypothesis (no bound string names it).

## Input / output
- `param_1` : `void*` out — receives the 0x24-byte reply struct (4 qwords @+0x00..+0x18 from `0x25ba80` mirror, plus u32 @+0x20 from `0x25baa0`).
- `param_2` : `const char*` — NUL-terminated request string (truncated at 0x100).
- `param_3` : `u32` — auxiliary request scalar (stored at `0x25bba8`).
- **return** : `u32` status word from `0x25bbc0` on success; `0` on any failure (mutex not owned, thread-ensure failed, or RPC error).

## Side effects
- WaitSema/PollSema on `DAT_002074a8`; SignalSema on all exit paths that acquired it.
- Sets command word `DAT_0020749c = 1`.
- First-use bind: if `DAT_002074c4 < 0`, spins `sif_rpc_bind(0x25bc00, 0x80000597)` until bound and server-ready word `iRam0025bc24 != 0`, then latches `DAT_002074c4 = 0`.
- Overwrites the shared request/reply DMA region `0x25ba80..0x25bbc0`.
- Emits trace lines when `ram0x00207490 > 0`.

## Important branches
- `PollSema != handle` → mutex not owned → return 0 (no work).
- `rpc_thread_ensure(1) != 0` → subsystem not ready → SignalSema, return 0.
- `DAT_002074c4 < 0` (not yet bound) → bind-and-wait loop (retries bind on negative, delay-spins ~0x100000).
- string copy hits index 0x100 → force-terminate (`uRam0025bba3 = 0`).
- `sif_rpc_call >= 0` → copy reply out + return status; else fall through → SignalSema, return 0.

## Constants & flags
- `0x80000597` — SIF-RPC **service id** bound by this wrapper (`0xffffffff80000597` = sign-extended).
- `0x100 (256)` — max request-string length.
- `300` — fixed send-buffer size.
- `0x24 (36)` — reply struct size copied to caller.

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
// EE-side synchronous SIF-RPC "resolve/lookup" request to IOP service 0x80000597.
// [inferred] request is a name/URL string; reply is a 36-byte address/result record.
u32 net_rpc_resolve_request(void *out_result /*[0x24]*/, const char *req_str, u32 aux)
{
    net_rpc_lock();                                   // FUN_00100590 (WaitSema DAT_002074a8)
    if (PollSema(DAT_002074a8) != DAT_002074a8)       // must own the net-RPC mutex
        return 0;

    g_net_cmd = 1;                                    // DAT_0020749c
    ReferThreadStatus(g_rpc_thread, &g_rpc_thread_status);
    if (rpc_thread_ensure(1) != 0) {                  // FUN_00100d78
        SignalSema(DAT_002074a8);
        return 0;
    }
    sif_dma_sync(0);                                  // FUN_00115300

    if (svc597_bound_flag < 0) {                      // DAT_002074c4, first use
        while (1) {
            while (sif_rpc_bind(&client_597, 0x80000597, 0) < 0) {
                if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_bind_retry);
                delay_spin(0x100000);
            }
            if (client_597.server_ready != 0) break;  // iRam0025bc24
            delay_spin(0x100000);
        }
        svc597_bound_flag = 0;
    }

    // marshal request string into shared DMA buffer @0x25baa4 (<= 0x100 bytes)
    char *dst = (char*)0x25baa4;
    int i = 0;
    if ((dst[0] = req_str[0]) != '\0')
        for (i = 1; i < 0x100 && (dst[i] = req_str[i]) != '\0'; i++) {}
    if (i == 0x100) dst[-1 /*0x25bba3*/] = 0;         // hard-terminate

    req_hdr.buf_ptr = 0x25ba80;                        // uRam0025bba4
    req_hdr.aux     = aux;                             // uRam0025bba8
    dcache_wb(0x25ba80, 300);                           // FUN_00115250

    long rc = sif_rpc_call(&client_597, 0, 0,
                           /*send*/0x25ba80, 300,
                           /*recv*/0x25bbc0, 4, 0);
    if (rc >= 0) {
        memcpy(out_result, (void*)0x25ba80, 0x24);      // 4 qwords + trailing u32
        u32 status = *(u32*)0x25bbc0;
        SignalSema(DAT_002074a8);
        return status;
    }
    SignalSema(DAT_002074a8);
    return 0;
}
```

## Evidence
- Raw decompile FUN_00100838.c (semaphore-guarded bind→marshal→call→copy structure).
- Sibling wrappers share the exact prologue/bind idiom; primitive names from `00115c68_sif_rpc_call.md`, `00115a98` bind.
- Confidence **Medium**: RPC mechanics firm; service-0x597 semantics (resolve/DNAS vs other) is a Hypothesis — no bound string. Runtime-unvalidated.

## Remaining uncertainty
Exact meaning of service 0x80000597 and the 0x24-byte reply layout; whether `param_3` is a flags word or length.

## Behavioral explanation
Same net-RPC family as 0x00100838, but a **bind/attach-only** operation for **service 0x80000595** (client node `0x208650`). It stashes `param_1` as the command word, ensures the RPC thread, and — if the service is not yet bound (`DAT_002074bc < 0`) — spins `sif_rpc_bind` until the server-ready word is non-zero, latches the bound-flag, and returns success. No `sif_rpc_call` is issued: this is the lazy **channel-open / readiness gate** for service 0x595 (called before the request wrappers that reuse it).

## Input / output
- `param_1` : `u32` command/context word (stored to `DAT_0020749c`).
- **return** : `1` if the service is bound & ready (already-bound fast path or after successful bind); `0` on failure (mutex not owned, or thread-ensure failed).

## Side effects
- WaitSema/SignalSema on `DAT_002074a8`; `DAT_0020749c = param_1`.
- On first use latches `DAT_002074bc = 0` after the bind+ready spin.
- Trace on `ram0x00207490 > 0`.

## Important branches
- `PollSema != handle` → not owner → if debug, trace `0x24e998` and return 0; else return 0.
- `rpc_thread_ensure(1) != 0` → SignalSema, return 0.
- `DAT_002074bc >= 0` (already bound) → **fast return 1** (does not re-bind, does not SignalSema — the fast path returns while still holding the mutex per decompile).
- else bind loop: retry `sif_rpc_bind(0x208650, 0x80000595)` on negative; break when `DAT_00208674 != 0`; latch flag; return 1.

## Constants & flags
- `0x80000595` — SIF-RPC service id for this channel.

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
// Lazy bind / readiness gate for IOP SIF-RPC service 0x80000595.
u32 net_rpc_bind_svc595(u32 ctx)
{
    net_rpc_lock();
    if (PollSema(DAT_002074a8) != DAT_002074a8) {
        if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_notowner, ctx, g_net_cmd);
        return 0;
    }
    g_net_cmd = ctx;                                  // DAT_0020749c
    ReferThreadStatus(g_rpc_thread, &g_rpc_thread_status);
    if (rpc_thread_ensure(1) != 0) { SignalSema(DAT_002074a8); return 0; }
    sif_dma_sync(0);

    if (svc595_bound_flag >= 0)                        // DAT_002074bc: already up
        return 1;                                     // (fast path, mutex still held)

    while (1) {
        while (sif_rpc_bind(&client_595, 0x80000595, 0) < 0) {
            if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_bind_retry);
            delay_spin(0x100000);
        }
        if (client_595.server_ready != 0) break;      // DAT_00208674
        delay_spin(0x100000);
    }
    svc595_bound_flag = 0;
    return 1;
}

Note the decompiled already-bound path returns 1 without SignalSema — likely a decompiler artifact of an early-return that keeps the lock for the follow-up request wrapper, or a genuine held-lock handoff; unverified.

**Referenced strings:** trace fmts 0x24ea38, 0x24ea58
**Referenced opcodes:** —
**State vars:** ram0x00207490 (debug gate), uRam0025ba18 (RPC-owner thread id)

## Behavioral explanation
Master **init / terminate** entry for the whole net-RPC subsystem. Binds the **control service 0x80000592** (client `0x25bc28`), records the calling thread as the RPC owner, resets every per-service bound-flag to "unbound" (`0xffffffff`), then sends a single 4-byte **mode word** (`param_1`) and reads a 0x10-byte reply. The reply carries a service-availability code and two sub-status words that are decoded into a coarse return (1 = ok, 2 = degraded/partial). When `param_1 == 5` it takes the **teardown** path: releases resources (`net_rpc_teardown`) and invalidates the three service semaphore handles; otherwise (init modes) it unlocks via `net_rpc_lock`/`net_rpc_unlock_all`.

## Input / output
- `param_1` : `s32` mode/command. Observed decode: `5` = terminate; other in-range values = init/query. (The guard `((p<0)||(p<2))||(p!=5)` selects the "not the 5-terminate" branch — i.e. teardown runs **only** when `param_1 == 5`.)
- **return** : `0` on RPC failure or thread-ensure failure; `1` ok; `2` degraded (a sub-status word's high bits `< 2`).

## Side effects
- Sets `uRam0025ba18 = GetThreadId()` (RPC owner), `DAT_002074a4 = 1` (busy), bumps `DAT_002074d4` (generation).
- Resets bound-flags to `-1`: `DAT_002074c0/c4/bc/cc/c8/d0`; `DAT_002074b8 = 0`.
- Binds & waits control service ready (`iRam0025bc4c != 0`), latches `DAT_002074d0 = 0`.
- `sif_rpc_call` control fno with the mode word; clears busy `DAT_002074a4 = 0`.
- Teardown path (mode 5): `net_rpc_teardown()`, then `DAT_002074a8 = DAT_002074ac = DAT_002074a0 = 0xffffffff` (invalidate semaphores).
- `DAT_2020868c == 0xfe` → sets `ram0x00207490 = 1` (enable debug verbosity).

## Important branches
- `rpc_thread_ensure2(1) != 0` → return 0 (subsystem down).
- bind-spin control service 0x592 until ready.
- `sif_rpc_call < 0` → `DAT_002074a4 = 0`, return 0.
- reply avail code `DAT_2020868c`: `0xff` = fully ok (skip); `0xfe` = enable debug; else inspect sub-status `DAT_20208684` / `DAT_20208688` — if either's `>>8 < 2`, downgrade return to `2`.
- `param_1 == 5` → teardown + invalidate semaphores; else → `net_rpc_lock()/net_rpc_unlock_all()` (normal init release).

## Constants & flags
- `0x80000592` — control service id.
- mode `5` — terminate/shutdown.
- reply avail `0xff` ok / `0xfe` ok+enable-debug / other = decode sub-status.
- return `0` fail / `1` ok / `2` degraded.

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
// Master init/terminate for the EE net-RPC subsystem (control service 0x80000592).
// param_1 == 5 => shutdown; other values => init/query.
u32 net_rpc_init_term(int mode)
{
    if (rpc_thread_ensure2(1) != 0) return 0;
    sif_dma_sync(0);

    g_rpc_owner_tid = GetThreadId();                  // uRam0025ba18
    g_rpc_busy      = 1;                              // DAT_002074a4
    g_rpc_generation++;                               // DAT_002074d4
    svc_bound_59a = svc_bound_597 = svc_bound_595 =
    svc_bound_593 = svc_bound_59c = ctrl_bound = -1;  // c0,c4,bc,cc,c8,d0
    g_rpc_flag_b8 = 0;

    while (1) {                                        // bind control svc, wait ready
        while (sif_rpc_bind(&ctrl_client, 0x80000592, 0) < 0) {
            if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_ctrl_bind_retry, rc, g_rpc_generation);
            delay_spin(0x100000);
        }
        if (ctrl_client.server_ready != 0) break;     // iRam0025bc4c
        delay_spin(0x100000);
    }
    ctrl_bound = 0;                                   // DAT_002074d0

    ctrl_cmd_word = mode;                             // iRam0025bc80
    dcache_wb(&ctrl_cmd_word, 4);
    long rc = sif_rpc_call(&ctrl_client, 0, 0,
                           &ctrl_cmd_word, 4, ctrl_reply/*0x208680*/, 0x10, 0);
    if (rc < 0) { g_rpc_busy = 0; return 0; }

    u32 ret = 1;
    u8 avail = ctrl_reply.avail;                       // DAT_2020868c
    if (avail != 0xff) {
        if (avail == 0xfe) {
            dbg = 1;                                   // ram0x00207490
        } else {
            if (asr8(ctrl_reply.sub0) < 2 ||           // DAT_20208684
                asr8(ctrl_reply.sub1) < 2)             // DAT_20208688
                ret = 2;                               // degraded
        }
    }
    g_rpc_busy = 0;

    if (mode != 5) {                                   // init release
        net_rpc_lock();
        net_rpc_unlock_all();                          // FUN_001007c0
    } else {                                           // terminate
        if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_teardown);
        net_rpc_teardown();                            // FUN_00100648
        sema_A = sema_B = sema_ctrl = 0xffffffff;      // invalidate handles
    }
    return ret;
}
```

## Behavioral explanation
Synchronous control RPC on **service 0x8000059a** (client `0x25bc50`, shared with 0x59c). Sends a single 4-byte op word (`param_1`) and returns the 4-byte reply. Mutex/thread-guarded like the family; binds lazily via the shared bound-flag `DAT_002074c8`. This is the **fallback target** invoked by `net_rpc_ctl_svc59c` (0x00101580) when the preferred 0x59c channel does not come ready in time.

## Input / output
- `param_1` : `s32` op/control code (marshalled into `0x25bc90`).
- **return** : reply word `DAT_20208680` on success; on failure `6` (generic error), or `0xffffffff` specifically when `param_1 == 8`.

## Side effects / branches
- Entry trace `0x24ea68`; WaitSema/SignalSema `DAT_002074ac`.
- `PollSema != handle` → return `6` (or `-1` if op 8).
- `rpc_thread_ensure2(1) != 0` → SignalSema; return `6`/`-1`.
- `DAT_002074c8 < 0` → bind-spin `sif_rpc_bind(0x25bc50, 0x8000059a)` until ready word `iRam0025bc74 != 0`; latch `0`.
- `sif_rpc_call >= 0` → return reply word; else SignalSema and return `6`/`-1`(op 8).

## Constants & flags
- `0x8000059a` — SIF-RPC service id.
- return `6` — default/error sentinel; `0xffffffff` — error for op `8`.

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
// Synchronous single-word control op on IOP SIF-RPC service 0x8000059a.
u32 net_rpc_ctl_svc59a(int op)
{
    if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_ctl59a_enter);
    net_rpc_lock();
    u32 fail = (op == 8) ? 0xffffffff : 6;
    if (PollSema(DAT_002074ac) != DAT_002074ac) return fail;

    if (rpc_thread_ensure2(1) != 0) { SignalSema(DAT_002074ac); return fail; }
    sif_dma_sync(0);

    if (svc59x_bound_flag < 0) {                       // DAT_002074c8 (shared 59a/59c)
        while (1) {
            while (sif_rpc_bind(&client_59x, 0x8000059a, 0) < 0) {
                if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_bind_retry);
                delay_spin(0x100000);
            }
            if (client_59x.server_ready != 0) break;   // iRam0025bc74
            delay_spin(0x100000);
        }
        svc59x_bound_flag = 0;
    }

    ctl_arg = op;                                       // uRam0025bc90
    dcache_wb(&ctl_arg, 4);
    long rc = sif_rpc_call(&client_59x, 0, 0, &ctl_arg, 4, ctl_reply/*0x208680*/, 4, 0);
    if (rc >= 0) {
        if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_ctl_ok);
        u32 v = ctl_reply.word;                         // DAT_20208680
        SignalSema(DAT_002074ac);
        return v;
    }
    SignalSema(DAT_002074ac);
    return fail;
}
```

## Behavioral explanation
Preferred variant of the single-word control op, on **service 0x8000059c** (same client node `0x25bc50`, own mutex `DAT_002074b0`, own reply buffer `0x208f00`). Structurally identical to `net_rpc_ctl_svc59a` but with a **bounded bind wait**: the bind-readiness spin retries at most ~0x11 (17) times, and if the 0x59c server never signals ready it **releases the mutex and delegates to `net_rpc_ctl_svc59a(param_1)`** (the 0x59a fallback). Returns the reply word, or `6` / `-1`(op 8) on error.

## Input / output
- `param_1` : `s32` op/control code.
- **return** : reply word `DAT_20208f00`; else `6`, or `0xffffffff` when `param_1 == 8`; or whatever the 0x59a fallback returns.

## Important branches
- `PollSema(DAT_002074b0) != handle` → skip to failure (`6`/`-1`).
- bind-ready spin bounded by counter `iVar2`: when `iVar2 > 0x10` **and** not yet ready → `SignalSema(DAT_002074b0)` + `return net_rpc_ctl_svc59a(op)` (fallback).
- ready (`iRam0025bc74 != 0`) → latch `DAT_002074c8 = 0`, proceed.
- `sif_rpc_call >= 0` → return reply word (`DAT_20208f00`); else SignalSema + `6`/`-1`.

## Constants & flags
- `0x8000059c` — SIF-RPC service id (preferred channel).
- retry cap `0x10` (17 iterations) before fallback.
- return `6` default / `0xffffffff` for op `8`.

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
// Preferred single-word control op on service 0x8000059c; falls back to 0x59a if 59c not ready.
u32 net_rpc_ctl_svc59c(int op)
{
    if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_ctl59c_enter);
    net_rpc_lock();
    if (PollSema(DAT_002074b0) == DAT_002074b0) {
        sif_dma_sync(0);
        int tries = 0;
        if (svc59x_bound_flag < 0) {                    // DAT_002074c8
            while (1) {
                while (sif_rpc_bind(&client_59x, 0x8000059c, 0) < 0) {
                    if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_bind_retry);
                    delay_spin(0x100000);
                }
                bool over = (tries > 0x10);
                if (client_59x.server_ready != 0) break; // iRam0025bc74
                tries++;
                if (over) {                              // 59c never came ready
                    SignalSema(DAT_002074b0);
                    return net_rpc_ctl_svc59a(op);       // fallback to 0x59a
                }
                delay_spin(0x100000);
            }
            svc59x_bound_flag = 0;
        }
        ctl_arg = op;                                    // uRam0025bc90
        dcache_wb(&ctl_arg, 4);
        long rc = sif_rpc_call(&client_59x, 0, 0, &ctl_arg, 4, ctl_reply_c/*0x208f00*/, 4, 0);
        if (rc >= 0) {
            if (dbg) net_tracef(FMT_ctl_ok);
            u32 v = ctl_reply_c.word;                    // DAT_20208f00
            SignalSema(DAT_002074b0);
            return v;
        }
        SignalSema(DAT_002074b0);
    }
    return (op == 8) ? 0xffffffff : 6;
}
```

| field | value |
|---|---|

**Callers:** — (indirect; invoked as a parser/stream callback)
**Callees:** FUN_00113b10 (stream_read), FUN_00113b48 (stream_write), FUN_00113c50 (sink_putc), FUN_00114a90 (net_panicf)
**Referenced globals:** —
**Referenced strings:** panic fmts 0x24fd20 (overflow), 0x24fd48 (read err), 0x24fd60 (write err), 0x24fd78 (residual)
**Referenced opcodes:** —
**State vars:** —

## Behavioral explanation
An **event-driven stream-filter callback** over a small descriptor object (`param_3`, a word array). It accumulates up to **0x140 (320) bytes** in an internal buffer and forwards them to a byte sink, and drains a producer's bytes out to a transport fd. `param_1` selects the event; `param_2` is a length/argument. The 320-byte cap + byte-at-a-time sink forwarding is characteristic of a **line/header accumulator** in a text protocol stage (HTTP/DNAS-style framing) sitting between a raw transport (`stream_read`/`stream_write` on the fd) and a consumer sink.

## Input / output
- `param_1` : `int` event selector — `1`/`2` = feed (data-in / flush), `3` = drain (write out), `4` = end/close. (`< 1` → no-op; other → no-op.)
- `param_2` : `ulong` length: for feed events, bytes to append, or `0` = flush the accumulator.
- `param_3` : `u32*` stream descriptor (see field map below).
- **return** : void (errors are fatal via `net_panicf`).

### Descriptor layout (`param_3[]`, inferred)
| idx | meaning |
|---|---|
| [0] | fd / transport handle |
| [1] | drain remaining length |
| [2] | accumulator fill count (0..0x140) |
| [3] | state flag (cleared to 0 on flush/end) |
| [4] | drain cursor (out ptr for write) |
| [5] | accumulator buffer base (cap 0x140) |
| [6] | sink object; byte written via `*(*(sink+0xc))` |

## Important branches
- **event 3 (drain/write):** `stream_write(fd, buf[4], buf[1]&0xffff)`; on success advance `buf[4] += n`, `buf[1] -= n`, return. On error → panic `0x24fd60`, then `buf[3] = 0`.
- **event 1/2, `param_2 == 0` (flush):** if header length word `*(u16*)buf[5] > 0xc`, loop from offset 0xc copying each byte into the sink (`**(sink+0xc) = b; sink_putc(sink)`); then `buf[2] = 0`.
- **event 1/2, `param_2 != 0` (append):** bounds `buf[2] + param_2 <= 0x140` else panic `0x24fd20`; `stream_read(fd, buf[5]+buf[2], param_2&0xffff)`; on `<0` panic `0x24fd48`; `buf[2] += n`.
- **event 4 (end):** if `buf[1] != 0` (unconsumed residual) panic `0x24fd78`; then `buf[3] = 0`.
- default / `param_1 < 1` / `param_1` other → return.

## Constants & flags
- `0x140 (320)` — accumulator capacity.
- `0xc (12)` — header prefix skipped on flush (first 12 bytes are a length/type header; payload starts at +0xc).
- events `1/2` feed, `3` drain, `4` end.

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
typedef struct {
    int   fd;            // [0]
    int   drain_len;     // [1]
    int   fill;          // [2]
    int   state;         // [3]
    u8   *drain_cur;     // [4]
    u8   *accum;         // [5]  (cap 0x140)
    void *sink;          // [6]  ( *(sink+0xc) -> byte sink cursor )
} stream_desc; // [inferred]

void stream_filter_event(int ev, ulong arg, stream_desc *d)
{
    if (ev == 3) {                               // drain to transport
        long n = stream_write(d->fd, d->drain_cur, d->drain_len & 0xffff);
        if (n >= 0) { d->drain_cur += n; d->drain_len -= n; return; }
        net_panicf(FMT_write_err);               // 0x24fd60
    } else if (ev < 4) {
        if (ev < 1) return;                      // no-op events
        if (arg == 0) {                          // FLUSH accumulator to sink
            u16 *hdr = (u16*)d->accum;
            if (*hdr > 0xc) {
                for (int i = 0xc; i < (int)*hdr; i++) {
                    *(u8*)(*(u8**)((char*)d->sink + 0xc)) = ((u8*)hdr)[i];
                    sink_putc(d->sink);          // FUN_00113c50
                }
            }
            d->fill = 0;
            return;
        }
        if ((u32)(d->fill + (int)arg) > 0x140)   // overflow guard
            net_panicf(FMT_overflow);            // 0x24fd20
        long n = stream_read(d->fd, d->accum + d->fill, arg & 0xffff);
        if (n < 0) net_panicf(FMT_read_err);     // 0x24fd48
        d->fill += n;
        return;
    } else if (ev == 4) {                        // END
        if (d->drain_len != 0) net_panicf(FMT_residual, d->drain_len); // 0x24fd78
    } else return;
    d->state = 0;                                // reached by ev==4 and write-err path
}
```

## Evidence
- Raw decompile FUN_00113cd0.c; four panic strings pin the error semantics; 0x140 cap and +0xc header offset are explicit.
- `stream_read`/`stream_write` roles inferred from the fd-first arg and advance-by-return pattern.
- Confidence **Medium** on structure; **Low** on the higher protocol it services (HTTP vs. DNAS XML vs. other). Runtime-unvalidated.

## Remaining uncertainty
Identity of the enclosing protocol and of the sink `+0xc` indirection; whether events 1 and 2 differ (both currently take the same `< 4` path).

| field | value |

| Original address | 0x00137578 |

| Confidence | High |
| Runtime validated | no |

**Callers:** — (indirect / init path)
**Callees:** FUN_00115a98 (sif_rpc_bind), FUN_00114ac8 (net_tracef)
**Referenced globals:** DAT_0021bfac (refcount), DAT_0021bf94 (service id var), client node 0x21c3f0, ready word 0x21c414
**Referenced strings:** fatal fmt 0x253dd0
**Referenced opcodes:** —
**State vars:** —

## Behavioral explanation
Idempotent, refcounted **one-time SIF-RPC bind** for a specific service. On the first call (refcount `DAT_0021bfac == 0`) it spins `sif_rpc_bind(client 0x21c3f0, service DAT_0021bf94)` until the server-ready word (`DAT_0021c414`) is non-zero; if the bind ever returns a hard error it logs `0x253dd0` and **halts forever** (fatal — this service is mandatory). It then increments the refcount so subsequent calls become no-ops. The service id lives in a variable (`DAT_0021bf94`) set elsewhere, so this is a generic "ensure my RPC channel is bound" gate used by the module's init.

## Input / output
- (none) : void → void.
- Effect: guarantees the RPC client `0x21c3f0` is bound & ready after return (or the machine has hung).

## Important branches
- `DAT_0021bfac != 0` (already bound) → skip straight to refcount bump.
- else bind-spin: `sif_rpc_bind < 0` → `net_tracef(0x253dd0)` + `while(1){}` **infinite hang** (fatal, no recovery).
- `DAT_0021c414 != 0` → break (ready); else short delay-spin (`for i=0xfffb; i!=-1; i-=4`).
- always: `DAT_0021bfac++`.

## Constants & flags
- service id — held in `DAT_0021bf94` (set by caller/init, not literal here).
- refcount `DAT_0021bfac` — 0 = unbound, >0 = bound N times.

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
// Bind a fixed IOP SIF-RPC service exactly once (refcounted); fatal-hang on bind error.
void rpc_bind_once(void)
{
    if (g_svc_refcount == 0) {                       // DAT_0021bfac
        while (1) {
            long rc = sif_rpc_bind(&g_svc_client, g_svc_id, 0); // 0x21c3f0, DAT_0021bf94
            if (rc < 0) {
                net_tracef(FMT_bind_fatal);          // 0x253dd0
                for (;;) { /* hang: service is mandatory */ }
            }
            if (g_svc_client.server_ready != 0) break; // DAT_0021c414
            for (int i = 0xfffb; i != -1; i -= 4) {} // delay spin
        }
    }
    g_svc_refcount++;
}
```

## Evidence
- Raw decompile FUN_00137578.c; refcount guard + fatal infinite-loop-on-error are explicit and unambiguous.
- Confidence **High** on structure; identity of the bound service unresolved (id is a runtime variable). Runtime-unvalidated.

## Remaining uncertainty
Which IOP service `DAT_0021bf94` holds and who sets it before the first call.

**Callers:** — (indirect / init path)
**Callees:** FUN_001f62c8 (netlib_preinit), FUN_00115300 (sif_dma_sync), FUN_00115a98 (sif_rpc_bind), FUN_00115c68 (sif_rpc_call), FUN_00114ac8 (net_tracef)
**Referenced globals:** DAT_0024e8ec / DAT_0024e8f0 (two semaphores), client node 0x390900, send buf 0x390980, reply buf 0x391ec0, ready word 0x390924
**Referenced strings:** fatal fmt 0x25b670, version-fail fmts 0x25b688, 0x25b6b0
**Referenced opcodes:** —
**State vars:** —

## Behavioral explanation
Initializes an IOP network library over SIF-RPC and performs a **version handshake**. On first entry it creates two semaphores (a mutex `DAT_0024e8ec` init=1, and a signal `DAT_0024e8f0` init=0), runs `netlib_preinit`, then under the mutex binds **service 0x80000400** (client `0x390900`) and issues RPC **fno 0xfe** (a "hello"/query) with a 0x30-byte request and a 0xC-byte reply. The reply is three words: `[0]` = handle/version returned to caller, `[1]` and `[2]` = the library's build/version numbers, which are gated against **minimums 0x20a and 0x20e**. If either is too old it returns a distinct negative error; a too-old library is rejected rather than used.

## Input / output
- (none) : void → `int`.
- **return** : reply word 0 (`iRam00391ec0`, a handle/version) on success; negatives on failure:
  - `rc - 100` if the RPC itself failed (`rc < 0`),
  - `-0x78 (-120)` if `reply[1] < 0x20a`,
  - `-0x79 (-121)` if `reply[2] < 0x20e`.

## Side effects
- One-time: `CreateSema` ×2 into `DAT_0024e8ec` (mutex, init/max=1) and `DAT_0024e8f0` (signal, init=0/max=1).
- `WaitSema/SignalSema(DAT_0024e8ec)` bracketing the RPC.
- Bind-spin service 0x80000400 until ready word `iRam00390924 != 0`; on bind hard-error logs `0x25b670` and **hangs forever** (fatal).
- On version failure clears the ready word (`iRam00390924 = 0`) so a later retry re-binds.

## Important branches
- `DAT_0024e8ec < 0` (semaphores not yet created) → create both.
- bind `sif_rpc_bind < 0` → fatal log + infinite hang.
- `sif_rpc_call < 0` → `iRam00390924 = 0`; return `rc - 100`.
- `reply[1] (iRam00391ec4) < 0x20a` → log `0x25b688`; clear ready; return `-0x78`.
- else `reply[2] (iRam00391ec8) < 0x20e` → log `0x25b6b0`; clear ready; return `-0x79`.
- else → return `reply[0]`.

## Constants & flags
- `0x80000400` — SIF-RPC service id (netlib).
- fno `0xfe` — init/hello query.
- `0x20a` / `0x20e` — minimum version numbers for reply words 1 and 2.
- errors `-100` (RPC), `-0x78` (ver1 too old), `-0x79` (ver2 too old).

## Corrected reconstruction
```c
// Init an IOP net library (SIF-RPC service 0x80000400) with a version handshake.
int netlib_init_version_gate(void)
{
    if (g_netlib_mutex < 0) {                            // DAT_0024e8ec: first time
        sema_attr a;
        a.init = 1; a.max = 1;  g_netlib_mutex  = CreateSema(&a);  // mutex
        a.init = 0; a.max = 1;  g_netlib_signal = CreateSema(&a);  // signal (DAT_0024e8f0)
    }
    netlib_preinit(0, 0, 0);                             // FUN_001f62c8
    WaitSema(g_netlib_mutex);
    sif_dma_sync(0);

    while (1) {                                          // bind service, wait ready
        if (sif_rpc_bind(&netlib_client, 0x80000400, 0) < 0) {
            net_tracef(FMT_bind_fatal);                 // 0x25b670
            for (;;) {}                                 // hang
        }
        if (netlib_client.server_ready != 0) break;     // iRam00390924
        delay_spin(0x100000);
    }

    long rc = sif_rpc_call(&netlib_client, 0xfe, 0,
                           send/*0x390980*/, 0x30, reply/*0x391ec0*/, 0xC, 0);
    SignalSema(g_netlib_mutex);

    if (rc < 0) {
        netlib_client.server_ready = 0;
        return (int)rc - 100;
    }
    if (reply.ver1 < 0x20a) {                            // iRam00391ec4
        net_tracef(FMT_ver1_old);                       // 0x25b688
        netlib_client.server_ready = 0;
        return -0x78;
    }
    int ret = reply.handle;                              // iRam00391ec0
    if (reply.ver2 < 0x20e) {                            // iRam00391ec8
        net_tracef(FMT_ver2_old);                        // 0x25b6b0
        netlib_client.server_ready = 0;
        return -0x79;
    }
    return ret;
}
```

## Evidence
- Raw decompile FUN_001f58b8.c; the two version gates (0x20a/0x20e) and their distinct error codes are explicit; fno 0xfe and the fatal bind-hang are explicit.
- Confidence **High** on structure; Medium on which IOP library (id 0x80000400) — no bound string. Runtime-unvalidated.

## Remaining uncertainty
Identity of service 0x80000400 and the 0x30-byte request contents; meaning of reply words 1/2 beyond "version >= threshold".
